After the death of Ahaz, king of Judah, his son Hezekiah reigned for 29 years; he lived 54 years. In the 6th year of his reign, Sennacherib ascended the throne in Babylon and reigned for 9 years. Then he made an expedition against Jerusalem, and he had as secretary Bocht-Nassar. * But the Lord, answering the prayer and the supplications that King Hezekiah had addressed to Him, caused 185,000 men of his army to perish. His history is written in the Book of the Kings of Israel.

When Sennacherib had fled, his son Esarhaddon, seeing * that his troops had been exterminated, seized him * and put him to death; then he reigned after him for three years.

In the 15th year of the reign of Hezekiah, Merodach-Baladan reigned for 48 years. History of King Hezekiah. It is written that King Hezekiah gave satisfaction to God in all his actions: he abolished the idols and their worship in the midst of his people, purified the Temple of the Lord of all defilement and impurity, sanctified it, and placed priests there, in the manner in which the prophet David had done.

In his time lived the prophets Isaiah and Micah. Isaiah prophesied about the advent of the Messiah. There was [also] the prophet Hosea who, throughout his life, exhorted the Israelites, turned them away from the worship and adoration of idols, and constrained himself to prayer and supplication * to obtain from his Lord that He might remove Sennacherib from his people: God answered his prayer and broke the anger of Sennacherib, king of Babylon.

* God wanted the prophet Hosea to know the hardness of the heart of the Israelites and their obstinacy toward Him. He therefore said to this prophet: "Go, marry a


[p. 58] {#po.11.p58}
فتحير هوشع النبى من قول الله له واغتم لذلك غما اوهنه! وانما اراد الله ان عام هوشع النبى قل صبرلا على مثل صبر ريه من بنى اسراييل وذلك ان الصحيح مني عند هوشع النبى انه كان جماعة منهم تسجدة للاوثان والاصنام سرا4 واخرون علانية فلذلك قال الله له ان يتزوج امراة مطلقة متعة9 علانية” والاخرى8 سرا زانية لكى لا يلج على ربّه فم ومكتوب ان حزتيا الملك الزم نفسه ان يكون بولا" لا يتزوج وذلك انه ذكر وعد الله لداوود النبى وحلفه له انه لا ينقص من زرعه ونسله من يجلى على مثير"! داوود ده الداهرين فتلت حزتيا الملك فى نفسه وقال ان هذ البركة التى بارك الله داوود النبى والموعد!! الذى وعد لا ينتقص من زرعه ونسله من يجلس على مير دهن الداهرين بركة تامة من عند الله ولن يرجع الله فيما وعد وفيما حاد به ولكن لان اباى واجدادى
repudiated woman who prostitutes herself publicly, and another woman who commits adultery in secret." The prophet Hosea was stunned by this order of God and conceived an overwhelming sorrow from it. But God wanted to make him know His lack of patience in comparison to that of the Lord toward the Israelites. The truth indeed, according to the prophet Hosea, is that several of them worshipped idols in secret, and the others publicly. That is why God ordered him to marry a repudiated woman who prostituted herself publicly, and another who committed adultery in secret, * to obtain from the prophet that he would no longer importune his Lord because of them. It is written * that King Hezekiah committed himself to devote himself exclusively to the service of God, without marrying, because he remembered the promise and the oath that God had made to the prophet David, namely that the race and the posterity of David would not cease to occupy his throne until the consummation of the ages. * King Hezekiah thought to himself and said: "The blessing that God has given to the prophet David and the promise that He has made to him, namely that his race and his posterity will not cease to occupy his throne until the consummation of the ages, - is a perfect blessing of God, who could never withdraw His promises nor His liberality. As my fathers and my ancestors married and had children, the blessing passed


[p. 59] {#po.11.p59}
كانوا يتزوجون ويولدون الاولاد فكاات البركة تنتقل من واحد الى واحد من الاب الى انه فيرث منبر! داوود النبى ولكنه اعم يجلس بولا ولا يتزوج فيدوم له ملكهة والجلون على منبى داوود النبى دهر الداهرين3 ولم يكن حزقيا الملك+ علم ان لهذا القولة الذى قال الله لداوود النبى تاويل9 غير الذى ظنه وتاقله7 فى نفسه؟ فد ذلك بعث الله اليه9 اشعيا النبى9! وقال له اذهب وقل!1 لحزقيا الملك ان! يوصٌى على بيته فانه يموت ولا يحبى يعنى بذلك ان الناس _يموتون وبحيون .يوم القيامة فاما هو فيموت ولا بحا حيث ظن فى نفسه انه بيقا ده الداهرين! فمرض حزقا الملك ساعة قال له اشعيا النبى وانبى اليه قول الرتّ4 فلما صار فى فراشه بكى بكاه شديدا بحزن وجل ييصق"! على الحابط ويقول تت لبذا التراب9! الوسخ النى صرت منه فظنتت7! الى ابا الى دهن الداهرين ثم انه ذكر الله فعلاته8! وبا كان منه فى ارضايه ربّه فل الله صلاته9! وبعث اليه اشعيا النبى ثانية وقال له اذهب الى حزتيا الملك فقل له ان الرب قد
from one to the other, from father to son, who thus inherited the throne of the prophet David." And the prophet Hezekiah imagined that, if he remained a virgin * and did not marry, he would reign and occupy the throne of the prophet David until the consummation of the ages. || had not understood that one had to interpret otherwise than he had imagined, the word addressed by God to the prophet David. But in the meantime God sent him the prophet Isaiah saying to the latter: "Go and tell King Hezekiah to make his will in favor of his own, for he is going to die and will live no more"; * as if He were saying: "People will die and will live again on the day of the Resurrection; as for him, he will die and will live no more, because he imagined that he would remain [alive] until the end of the ages." And King Hezekiah fell ill, as soon as the prophet Isaiah had told him and reported the word of the Lord. On his bed, he began to weep bitter tears, prey to sadness. || spat against the wall, saying: "Fie for this filthy mud from which I draw my origin! * and I believed I would live until the consummation of the ages!" Then * he reminded God of his actions and what he had done to satisfy his Lord. God accepted his prayer and sent him the prophet Isaiah for the second time saying to the latter: "Go to


[p. 60] {#po.11.p60}
زادك! على حياتك خمس عشر سنة وهذه لك علامة ان احنت ان تتقدم الشمس الى قدامه خمسة عشر درحة وان احببت ان تناخر الى خلفه خمس عشر درحة من درج اخاز ابيه فقال حزقيا الملك فى نفسه لبت هذه بعجية ان تتقدم الشمس الى قدامه4 لانه سبيل سير الشمس وطريقة حركتها ولكن لترجع الى خافبا هذه الخمس عش درحة فقال لاشعيا البى اسال ان ترجع الشمس الى خلف خمسة عشر درحة فلما صارت الشمس9 فى كبد السمآ نصف النبار وقت الزوال تمام ست ساعات تامات8 رجعت الشمس “من وسط السما الى افق المشرق فصارت فى وقت طلوعبا صلاة" الغداة
King Hezekiah and say to him: 'The Lord has added fifteen years to your life; and here is the proof: ask as you wish that the sun, on the dial of your father Ahaz, advance fifteen degrees, or that it recede fifteen degrees.' Then King Hezekiah said to himself: 'It would not be a * wonder for the sun to advance, since that is the path of its course and the law of its movement. Let it rather recede fifteen degrees!' He therefore said to the prophet Isaiah: 'I ask that the sun recede fifteen degrees.' Now the sun, which was * then in the middle of the sky, at noon, at the moment when it descends, at the end of the sixth hour, retraced its steps toward the side of the Orient as if it were at the moment of its rising, at the hour of the morning prayer.
وما احسن نما دبر حزقيا الملك فى نفسه ومسلته ان ترجع الشمس الى خلفها لحالتين اما واحدة فانه0! ليس سبيل حركتها وسجرى طرقتها ولثانية رسا كان "النان فى اشغاليم واعمالهم واحاديثهم فيذهب من النهار ساعات !! فلا يحسون يا فلما صارت الشمس فى وسط السمآ وتمت ست ساعات واحتاج الناس فى ذلك الوقت الى الطعام والصناع
I do not see clearly, for two reasons, what King Hezekiah was thinking when he asked that the sun recede: firstly, it is not the path of its movement nor the line of its route; secondly, often people, in their work, their occupations, or their conversations, pass several hours of the day without noticing it; but it was when the sun was in the middle of the sky, at the end of the sixth hour, it was when people needed * to eat, the workers, the laborers, and others,


[p. 61] {#po.11.p61}
واصحاب الاعمال وغيرهم الى الراحة رجت الشمس! من ست ماعات من نهار فصارت فى وقت مطلعها صلوة العداة فحينيذ عرف ذلك وتبينه جميع البش وحست .به الهايم فضلا عن الناس4 ثم لا احد يشغى له ان بظن ان الشمس رجت من درحة وسط السما الى درحة الطالع دون يجوع الفلك بكلبته لكنا نقول ان الفلك باسرلا اندفع فرجع بجميع كواكبه وانواره والكواكب فى مواضعبا فرجع من درحة وسط الما الى افق المشرق فنقول انه لو رجت الشمس من درجة وسط السما الى درجة الطالع؟ لرجعمت الثاشة بروج وتغيرت الازمنة وفصول السنة الارعة فلو كان فصل الربيع حول فصار فصل الفية ولو كان غسل النبشء تحول: فصان "فصل الخريف: ولو كان: فصل: البشيف تحول فصار فصل الثتا ولو كان فصل الشتا تحول فصار فصل الربيع فتغيرت الازمنة والفصول الارعة
to rest, that the sun returned on its steps, at such an hour, and came back to the moment of its rising, at the hour of the morning prayer. Then all men must have noticed it and observed it; * even the animals must have noticed it, not to mention men. Moreover, one must not imagine that the sun returned from the degree of the middle of the sky to the place (lit. degree) of its rising without the return of the entire * celestial sphere. Thus we say that the entire celestial sphere receded, returned with all its stars and its luminaries—the stars remaining in their places—and returned from the middle of the sky toward the side of the Orient. And we add that, if the sun returned from the degree of the middle of the sky to the place of its rising, the three signs of the Zodiac also returned on their steps and the four seasons of the year were changed: if it was the spring season, it changed into the summer season; * if it was the summer season, it changed into the autumn season; if it was the autumn season, it changed into the winter season, and if it was the winter season, it changed into the spring season; so that the four seasons changed.
وشغى؟ لك حفظك الله ان تحفظ مثل7 هذا وتعرفه قانه من غوامض العلم ودفاين اسرار. الحكمة وبثبين لك ذلك عند منلتك عنه علما الناس والمتعثين فى 'قراةٍ
* You must (God forbid!) retain and possess well these things which are part of the depths of science and the mysterious treasures of wisdom; you must have exact knowledge, when you question


[p. 62] {#po.11.p62}
الكتب وساير العلوم والاداب فتعرف ان كان فيهم احد يعرف مثل هذ الاسرار! فتفرح بمعرفتك بما تعلمه من دفاين اسرار العلم بما لا يعلمه غيرك من الحكما والطارقة والاساقفة وغيرهم ومكتوب ان مردوخ بلدان3 ملك بابل بعث هدايا وكرامات وقرابين الى حزقيا الملك4 عند فحصهة عن رجوع الشمس من وسط السما الى افق المشرق وذلك ان المجوس يعبدون الشمس ولثار والما وساير الانوار فلما راى مردوخ الملك رجوع الشمس قال لاصحابه انى ارى الاهنا قد انهزم وشغى ان يكون اله اخرة اكبر منه نطحه فرد وهزمه الى خلفه ففحص عن ذلك فحيث تقرر عنده الخر عن حزقّا الملك وذكر نوةٌ بلعام القاصوم بينهم حيث قال يظبر كوكب من يعقوب وراس من اسراييل فعند ذلك هاب وفرق من حزقيا الملك فعث اليه القرابين والبدايا تكرمة له وتقريا اليه وفرقا منه فلما" قدم على حزقيا"! الملك رسل !1 مردوخ الملك بلبدايا
on this point the scholars and those who immerse themselves in the reading of books of science or literature. You will then see if there is a single one among them who knows such mysteries and you will rejoice to possess these hidden treasures of science, which no one, except you, knows, neither the sages, nor the patriarchs, nor the bishops, nor other men. It is written that Merodach-Baladan, king of Babylon, sent King Hezekiah presents, gifts, and offerings, when he had observed that the sun had returned from the middle of the sky * toward the side of the Orient. The Magi worship the sun, fire, water, and all luminous bodies; * thus King Merodach, witness to the return of the sun, said to his companions: 'I see that our god has been routed: there must exist another god more powerful who has struck it, pushed it back, and made it retreat.' Then he inquired about it. And when he knew what had happened to King Hezekiah and learned the prophecy of Balaam the magician who had said: 'A star shall come out of Jacob and a leader out of Israel,' he was seized with fear and he was afraid of King Hezekiah; * that is why he sent him gifts and presents, in testimony of his veneration, to seek his favor because he feared him. When the ambassadors of the


[p. 63] {#po.11.p63}
والقرابين افيض حزقيا فاخرج جميع انية البيت وعجايب ما كان فيه! فاراهم ذلك فغضب الله عليه وقال له حقا يقول الرب ان جميع ما أظبرت وافتخرت به ينتهب ويحمل3 الى بلاد المشرق ومن زرعك وننلك يسبون الى هنالك فند ذلك تزوج حزقيا الملك فولد له منشاة فلما صار منشا ابنه ابن اشة عشر سنة ملك بعد وفاة ابيه حزقيا فكان ملكه الخسة وخسينسنة وعاش اثمنية وستين سنةة وملك فى ذلك الزمان فى مدينة رومية فنوفوليوس ثلثة وارعين سنة7 وهو الذى زاد كانون الاخر9 وشباط! فى شبور الروم وذلك انه لم يكن للروم الى "ذلك الزمان الا عشر شور فانهم ذلك فكان كل شهر منها!! ستة وثاثين يوما وهو اتقن بمدينة رومية قطوليون! وهو العجب الاول من ” العجايب السبعة واحكمه ووسع حجرلا ومخادعه لانها كانت ضيقة
King Merodach arrived before King Hezekiah, with their offerings and their presents, the latter became proud; he displayed all the vessels of the temple and all the wonders it contained, * to show them to the envoys. Then God became angry with him and said to him: 'In truth, says the Lord: * Everything that you have shown and of which you have been proud, will be plundered and carried away into the lands of the Orient; your sons and your descendants will be led there into captivity.' In the meantime, King Hezekiah married and fathered Manasseh, who, after the death of his father Hezekiah, ascended the throne at the age of 13. His reign lasted 55 years, and he lived 68 years. At that time, in the city of Rome, (Numa) Pompilius reigned for 43 years. He added to the months of the Romans Kanun II (January) and Shebat (February); until that time the Romans had known only ten months; (understand this!) and each month was 36 days. He built and erected in the city of Rome the Capitol, * the first * of the seven wonders; he widened its hall and chambers, because they were narrow.


[p. 64] {#po.11.p64}
وفى سنة ثمانية عشر من ملك منشا سنا الى بابل من! كثرة حورا ونفاقه وميخالفة ريه؟ واعادته عبادة الاصنام والاوثان فصار بابل فى الحبس مسجونا الى تمام تسعة وثلثين سنة فصلا وتضرع الى ريه فرجع الى مملكته واخرج الصنم الذى كان عمل وكان له اربعة وجول الذى كان صيرل فى هيكل الرب ونفلا من البيكل واحرقه وصلاة8 منشا حسنة4 بحزن وتضرع الى ربّهة وهى مكتونة فى كتب النصادى والبود وفى سنة تسعة وثلثين من ملك منشا ملك ستحارب الصغير احد؟ وثلثين سنة وفى سنة اثنين وخسين من ملك منشا ملك بوذا نيت بوزنطية ' من بوذس الملك التى كات تسما قبل ذلك يقوموديا” وكان خسف با فمن بعد تسع ماية وسبعين سنة من نيان بوزس الملك مدينة بوزنطية حددها قطنطين الملك الكبير ابن هلائى ووستها وكبر بلاطها وملاعبها وحبطانها وسماها باسمه قسطنطينية؟
Manasseh, in the 18th year of his reign, was taken into captivity in punishment for his great injustice, his impiety, his opposition to the Lord, and the restoration of the worship of idols; he remained imprisoned in Babylon for 39 years. He addressed prayers and supplications to the Lord and he returned to his kingdom where he removed the four-faced idol he had made and placed in the temple of the Lord; he removed this idol from the temple and had it burned. The prayer of Manasseh is beautiful in its sadness and in the humble supplication that the king addresses to his Lord; it is inscribed in the books of the Christians and the Jews. In the 39th year of the reign of Manasseh, Sennacherib the Younger began his reign which lasted 31 * years. In the 52nd year of the reign of Manasseh, king of Judah, Byzantium * was built by King Byzos. It was previously called Nicomedia and had been swallowed up. Then, 970 years after the founding of the city of Byzantium by King Byzos, the Emperor Constantine the Great, son of Helena, restored it, enlarged it, built palaces, theaters, and surrounding walls there, and gave it his name, Constantinople (Qastantiniya).


[p. 65] {#po.11.p65}
فحيث مات منشا ملك! بعده امون ابنه اثنا عشر سنة وكانت حياته ارعة وعشرين بش

At that time, flourished the sage Alcman, who is called among the Arabs Logman. In the 5th year of the reign of Amon, his son Josiah was born.

From that * time, after 348 years, the prophecy concerning the Altar was realized. * After the death of Amon, his son Josiah reigned in his place for 31 years.
وفى تلك السنة ملك ببابل المجوس اثة وثلثين سنة8 ١ وفى سنة ستة من ملك يوشيا كان تنبا صفنيا؟_البى بن9! كوشى من سبط شمعون !1 وكان خلقيا الكاهن ابو ا ارما النبى وفى سنة ثلثة عشر من ملك يوشيا بدا أرما النبى فى نموته
In that year, the Mage ascended the throne of Babylon and reigned 33 years. In the 6th year of the reign of Josiah, * the prophet Zephaniah, son of Cushi, of the tribe of Simeon, was prophesying. In the * 3rd year of the reign of Eliakim, Nebuchadnezzar ascended the throne in Babylon and reigned for 45 years. The first year of his reign, he attacked Jerusalem, took into captivity a part of its inhabitants, and carried away all the utensils and all the vessels of the temple, according to the word that God had spoken to King Hezekiah, when the latter had been proud * of these [treasures] and had shown them to the envoys of King Merodach. Among these first captives that Nebuchadnezzar took, that year, there was Daniel with his companions, Hananiah, Azariah, and Mishael. When the Israelites were taken captive to Babylon, Nebuzaradan seized the high priest Zephaniah and other leaders and nobles of the people as well as Zedekiah, king of Judah, and he transported them to Babylon where he had them killed; he had the eyes of Zedekiah put out and his sons slaughtered; then he made him turn a hand mill. He was then 32 years old. After his death, his corpse was thrown behind the surrounding wall; and thus was fulfilled the prophecy that the Lord had made about him through the mouth of the prophet Jeremiah. * As for the Jews who remained in Jerusalem, they took the prophet Jeremiah and went down to Hophra, king of Egypt. * There, they killed the prophet Jeremiah who died and was buried. He had prophesied for * 38 years. His book contains 4,252 verses; the book of Kings contains 1,113 and the book of Chronicles 3,503.

A short time later, Nebuchadnezzar attacked the city of Tyre. He ordered his troops to pile stones into the sea to reach the city. But at this sight, the inhabitants of Tyre threw everything they had into the sea and fled on their ships. * Nebuchadnezzar took Hiram, king of Tyre, and returned. Hiram lived 50 years, during the reigns of the 21 kings of the tribe of Judah. When the troops of Nebuchadnezzar had left the city of Tyre, the land of Egypt fell into their hands. Nebuchadnezzar reigned 24 years after the destruction of Jerusalem and the burning of the temple. It is from the 22nd year of his reign that one begins to count the 70 years of the captivity of the Israelites * in Babylon.

After the death of Nebuchadnezzar, his son Evil-Merodach reigned for one year. In the 5th year of the captivity, Belshazzar ascended the throne; and it is in the first year of his reign that the prophet Daniel saw in a dream the beast that was rising from the sea. In the 2nd year of * his reign, Belshazzar held a feast and while he was sitting on a high seat, * he had the sacred vessels brought that had been taken from the Temple of the Lord, and drank from them. But he had barely drunk from these vessels, when a hand appeared on the wall opposite writing the misfortune that was happening to him and the punishment that awaited him. Here is what was written: 'He who counts the hairs of the head (God) * has counted and numbered'

his kingdom and torn his veil (deprived him of defense)." That same night Darius, the Mede and the Persian, entered his house, killed him, and annexed his kingdom to that of the Medes and Persians... Darius, son of Ahasuerus (Ahchîrâch), began to reign in the 28th year of the captivity. In the first year of this reign, the prophet Daniel offered a prayer, * because he saw clearly that the years were approaching and that the 70 years which God had fixed for their slavery in Babylon were drawing to an end. They had begun, in fact, in the thirteenth year of the reign of Josiah, the time when the prophet Jeremiah was making his prophetic debut. Having answered the prayer and supplications of Daniel, the Lord sent him the archangel Gabriel and announced to him what concerned the weeks, the destruction of Jerusalem, * and the kings who reigned at that time and who are mentioned in the book of the prophet Daniel. At that time, Daniel was thrown into the lions' den.

Cyrus the Persian arose. || killed Astyages, king of the Medes, and Darius, king of the Babylonians; then he established the Persian empire in the 31st year of the captivity of the Israelites. He reigned * 30 years. In the first year of his reign, he ordered 50,000 Israelite captives to be freed and set at liberty, and he sent them to Jerusalem. From that moment, he ordered them to rebuild the Temple of the Lord; but they were hindered by the peoples surrounding them until the end of the sixth year of Darius, son of Hystaspes, which is the 46th year of their captivity, * as it is written * in the Gospel of John. Let no one think, therefore, that the Jews took 46 years to rebuild this Temple.

After which the kingdom of Egypt ceased to exist until the 15th year of the reign of Darius.


[p. 76] {#po.11.p76}
وفى ذلك الزمان قطع فنسموس ! الحجر المتكلم وظن ان فيه اسرار البح فلما ملك قمبودس ملك المجوس ثلث سنين وسعة اشر ومن بعد ذلك ملك داربوس ابن .يستاسصف3 ستة وثاثين سنة وفى السنة اثانية من ملكه تنثث السعين سنة4 وفى سنة سئة من ملك داربوس تم بنيان بيت الربّ3 فى شهر ادار فى ثنائة
At that time, Fansamous (?) cut the stone that spoke, because he believed he would find inside * the mysteries of magic. After Cambyses, the Mage reigned for 3 years and 7 months; after the latter, Darius, son of Hystaspes, reigned * 36 years. In the 2nd year of his reign, the seventy years were completed; and in the 6th year, on the 3rd of the month of Adar (March), the construction of the Temple of the Lord was finished.
ايام منه وفى ذلك الزمان كان ديمقراطيس ؟ الفيلسوف
At that time, the philosopher Democritus flourished.
وحيث مات داربوس ملك كسروس انه بعد الذى7 هو اخشيراش ستة وعشرين سنة وفى ذلك الزمان كان ذوحوسيس المصوّر؟
After the death of Darius, his son Xerxes, that is to say Ahasuerus (Akhchirâch), reigned for 26 years. At that time, the painter Zeuxis distinguished himself.
وفى ذلك الزمان كانت استير الببودية ومرتخى9! ابن اخى ارما النبى ابن برخيا!! الكاهن وكان مرتخى عم استير! وكانا فى سبى بابل
At the same time, lived the Jewess Esther and Mordecai, nephew of the prophet Jeremiah, son of the high priest Berechiah. Mordecai was Esther's uncle. They were both captives in Babylon.


[p. 77] {#po.11.p77}
وفى ذلك الزمان تنا ملاخى ! النبى احد الاثنى عشر نيا وكان يتنا فى بلاد يهوذا فلما مات ملاخى لم يقم فى بنى أسراييل نبى معروف بعد ويكون جميع كتاب الاثنى عشر نيا ثلثة الف وستماية وثلثة وارجين اية ويكون كتاب استير
At that time, the prophet Malachi, one of the twelve prophets, flourished; he * prophesied in the land of Judah. After his death * no other known prophet arose among the Israelites. * The book of the Twelve Prophets contains in all 3,643 verses. The book of Esther contains 654 verses.
ستماية واربعة وخسين آي وفى سنة احدى عش من ملك اخشيراش الملك غلب على مدينة انيناس4 وغلب5 على مداين كثيرة وملك انه من بعده سنتين ونصف ومن9 بعد ذلك ملك ارطحششت الطويل اليدين وسمى اربوخ فملك احد7 وارسين سنة
In the 11th year of his reign, Xerxes (Akhchirâch) seized the city of Athens and many other cities. His son succeeded him for two and a half years, and he himself had as successor Artaxerxes Longimanus, nicknamed Ariyoukh, who reigned for 41 years.
وفى ذلك الزمان وقع حجر من السما فى انهار اوغش8 وكان اسمبها افسيس9؟ وق سنة سبعة من ملك ارطحثشت الملك امر عزرا"1 سفرا اعنى الكاتب!! ان يصعد الى بلاد يهوذا وبنى بيت *! المقدس فى رياسة كبنوت يوشع ابن يوذادق *! فارى 5! لعزرا هذا واوحى اليه فكتب جميع التورية والانبيا من الحفظ ظاهراً ولتب
At that time, a stone fell from the sky into the river Aegos (Augoch); its name was Afsis (AÏ&?). In the 7th year of his reign, King Artaxerxes ordered the scribe Ezra to go up to the land of Judah and rebuild Jerusalem; this was under the pontificate of the high priest Joshua, son * of Jozadak. * This was explained and revealed to Ezra. Ezra wrote the entire Torah and the Prophets from memory, for he knew it by heart; he also set himself to describe and recount how the people had


[p. 78] {#po.11.p78}
ايضا صف! وبخبر كيف ارتفع الشعب من بابل وعزراة هو كان الكاهن الرابع عشر من بعد هارون4 ويكون كتاب عزرا الفين وثمان ماية وثمان ايات
departed from Babylon. He was the fourteenth high priest after Aaron. The book of Ezra contains 2,808 verses.
وفى ذلك الزمان كان ابودقليوسنة الحكيم الذى كان من سقلية فذهب لينظر وحص عن نار كانت تنبع وتفور من مغارة فلما لم يق على دفين معرفتها رمى بنفسه فيها ونسى حذاا خارجا منها فافتضح وعرف انه ليس له كالذى كان يتخذه اصحابه وفى ذلك الزمان عرف ديمقراطيس وابقراط الاطيا وزنون وهرقليطوس ”* وفى ذلك الزمان بعث الروم الى مدينة اثيئاس فانتستخوا منها وكتبوا منباة اننى عش ناموس
At that time, lived the wise Empedocles of Sicily. He went to see and examine the fire that gushed in whirlwinds from a cavern. Unable to understand this mystery, he threw himself into the cavern; but he forgot his shoes outside. Thus he betrayed himself and made it known that he did not possess the qualities that his contemporaries had attributed to him. At the same time, Democritus and Hippocrates, who were physicians, distinguished themselves; Zeno and Heraclitus. It was then also that the Romans * sent messengers to the city of Athens: [these messengers] copied [the laws of the Athenians] * and wrote the XII Tables based on them.
وفى سنة عشرين من ملك ارطحشثت الملك بعث تنحميا !1 صاحب شرابه ان .يبنى بيت المقدس فلبث هناك الى تمام خسة وعشرين سنة من ملك ارطحشثت وكان
Artaxerxes, in the 20th year of his reign, sent Nehemiah, his cupbearer, to rebuild Jerusalem; Nehemiah remained in Jerusalem until the end of the 25th year of the reign


[p. 79] {#po.11.p79}
وزنر ارطحشثت الملك هامان العماليقى وكان يتذكر العداوة القديمة والحروب التى كانت بين بثى اسرابيل وين السالقة! ففظها عليهم وكان يطلب علاكهم واستفصال شعهم2 من الملك وفى تلك السنة تم بنيان بيت المقدس اسواتها وبلاطاتها وقصورها فى رياسة كبنوت يوبداع ابن اليشع وحيث صعد نحميا الى بيت المقدس لم يكن لليهود نار من نار القدس وفرقوا” ان يقريوا الى الله8 نار9 غريبة من احل انهم فى الايام التى سبوا الى بابل اخذوا نار مذبح القدس الرتٍ فطرحوها فى بير وكانت تلك الير !1 فى بعض"! قرى مدينة افامية3! واسم! القرية اصبين فامر نحميا صاحب شراب فحمل من شيانة! تلك البير وطينها من بعد اربعة وسبعين سنة كانت انار قد طرحت فيا6! فوضع ذلك17 الشيان والطين على الحطب فوق المذبح واستوقدت منه النار
of Artaxerxes. At that time lived the minister of King Artaxerxes, Haman the Agagite. He remembered the old enmity and the wars that had once separated the Israelites and the Amalekites; he held a grudge because of it. And he asked the king to destroy and exterminate the Jewish people. In that same year, the construction of Jerusalem was completed, with its streets, its palaces, its citadels: this was under the pontificate of the high priest Joiada, son of Eliashib. When Nehemiah had gone up to Jerusalem, the Jews had no sacred fire * and they did not dare to offer sacrifices to God with profane fire: * at the time when they had been taken captive to Babylon, they had taken the sacred fire from the altar of the Lord and had thrown it into a well that was in one of the suburbs of the city of Apamea; this suburb was called Ashîn (?). The cupbearer Nehemiah had mud and mire brought to him from this well, seventy-four years after the fire had been thrown into it; he placed this mud and mire on the wood * of the altar; and the fire ignited.
وفى ذلك الزمان ولد افلاطن 9! وعرف سقراطيس سفسطاى فى مدينة اثيئاس ومن
At that time, Plato was born and the sophist Socrates distinguished himself in the


[p. 80] {#po.11.p80}
اجل انه كان يتلعب فى الصبيان! اسقى قونيون فمات واقام افلاطن3 واظين افعاله4 وتعاليمه الردية المرجوسة وفى ذلك الزمان حيث اعمل هامان وزير الملك نفيه9 وفكرئه بان بحتال لجميع شعب بنى اسراييل7 فيبيدهم وستاصليم حينيذ لبت استين ومرتحى المنوح وصليا وتضرعا الى الله فرد الله كيد هامان9 العماليقى 9!. فى تحر وقتله ارطمحششت !! الملك وصليه
city of Athens. As he was corrupting the young men, they made him drink poison, and he died. * But he left Plato who divulged his actions as well as his depraved and infamous doctrine. At the same time, while Haman, the king's minister, was using all the resources of his mind to seek ruses to destroy and exterminate all the people of Israel, Esther and Mordecai covered themselves with sackcloth and addressed their prayers and supplications to God. God made the artifices of the Agagite Haman fall back * on his own head: Artaxerxes had him killed and crucified.
قصّة استير نت اخى مرتحى ابن اخى ارما النبى وهامان العماليقى وزير الملك ارطحششثت
History of Esther, niece of Mordecai, nephew of the prophet Jeremiah, and of Haman the Agagite, minister of King Artaxerxes.
مكتوب ان ارطحششت الملك هيّا صنعاا واعد فيه ا النعيم الكثيرا وجمع خاصّته15 وقرايبه وقواده وكان هامان العماليقى رس الوزرا4! الخاض بالملك والمقدم
It is written that King Artaxerxes prepared a great feast where he gathered all the delicacies; he summoned his notables, * his relatives, and the commanders of his


[p. 81] {#po.11.p81}
عند فلم يدع الملك شيا من النعيم وانواع اللبو الا بلغه فى .يومه ومجاسه ذلك! ففكر فى نفسه وقال انه لم يبق من لذته شى الا ان تتهيًا الملكة وشتى فتصيرة اليه فتسقيه وتسقى اصحابه فبعث اليها إن تتشكل وتتصنع وتاتيه الى مجله ومسجلس اصحابه فرةّت عليه اشنع ما يكون من الجواب واقطعه8 فغمّه ذلك .غم شديدا قاتسان7 جميع ما كان فيه من لذته فى يومه ذلك وفى جميع" ايام ملكه فانصرف عنه9؟ اصحابه وجلساوه حيث راوا!1 غته وحزنه! وشال انه امر بطرد! وشتى الملكة من ملكها ان تتخذ له وتختار من جميع من فى4! ملكه الف مراةةا وكان تحت يده فى ملكه ماية وسعة وعشرين مدينة وت 9! رسله ان يختار له افضل من يقدر”! عليه من النسا حسنًا وحمالاة! وعقلا فلما اخترت9! كانت استير الببودية بين" ثم امر ان بيختار من الالف امراة ماية امرانة من احسنين واحملين واعقلن فاختير !* منهن ماية
army. The Agagite Haman was his prime minister and his steward. At this feast, when the king had enjoyed to satiety all the delicacies and all the kinds of pleasures he could desire, he said to himself that there remained only one enjoyment at his disposal; it was that Queen Vashti should put on her most beautiful finery and present herself to give drink to him and his companions. Consequently, the king sent word to the queen to adorn herself with flowers and to paint her face to come to his banquet and that of his companions. But she gave him the vilest and most cutting of answers. * The king conceived such sorrow from it that he forgot all the pleasures of that day and of the years of his reign. Witnesses to his sorrow and his sadness, his companions and his friends left him. And it is said that he ordered Queen Vashti to be driven from his kingdom, and to choose for himself a thousand women, from among all those of his empire. * This empire contained 127 cities subject to his scepter. He therefore sent his messengers to choose for him the most remarkable women, as much as possible, * by their beauty, their grace, and their wit. When the choice of these women was settled, Esther the Jewess was among them.


[p. 82] {#po.11.p82}
امراة1 وكات استين فين فامر ان يختار من الماية مرة عشر من اكملين3 حا وجمالا وعقلا فاختير5 منهن عشرة؟ وكانت استير فبهن ثم امر ان يختار من العشرة ثلشة فاختير من العشرة لئة7 فكاات استير فيهن ثم امر ان .يختار من الثاثة افضابنّ واكملبن8 حسنا وحمالا وعقلا فاختير من الثلشة استير بنت اخى مرتحى لانه لم يكن فى النسا من يفوقها حسنا وجمالا وعقلا" فامر الملك ان. تصنع9! استير وتّا!! ثلثين يوما وتدخل! على الملك فلما دخات عليه وصارت ملكة مكان وشتى فند ذلك لازم مرتحى باب الملك فلم يكن يبرح من الباب وحزن هامان عند ذلك وغقه غعّا شديدا فاراد ان يحتال لارطحششت الملك وستا عليه4! ويدخل فى عقله فقال له فى مملكتك ايها الملك امة وهى شرذمة قليلة من شعب نى اسراييل فلم يزل بها حتى ابناع منه جميع من فى مداين ملكه من بنى اسرابيل بمال! معلوم قتمه له7! وكتب
The king then ordered to choose, from among them, the hundred most beautiful, most graceful, and most intelligent women. They chose these hundred women, and Esther was among them. From among these hundred women, he ordered to choose ten who were more particularly remarkable for beauty, grace, and wit. They chose ten, and Esther was among them. Again, he ordered to choose three women, from among these ten. They chose three, and Esther was still among them. * || He finally ordered to choose, from among these three, the one who surpassed all the others in beauty, grace, and wit. And, from among these three, they chose Esther, niece of Mordecai, for no woman surpassed her in beauty, * grace, and wit. The king therefore ordered Esther to beautify herself and prepare her finery for thirty days, after which she would enter to him. From the day she presented herself and became queen in place of Vashti, Mordecai remained at the king's gate and never left it. Haman, extremely afflicted and preoccupied by this attitude, used ruse and lies to gain ascendancy over King Artaxerxes; he said to him: "In your empire, O king, there exists a people or rather a small troop of Israelites." And he did not cease to insist until he had bought from the king, for a determined sum of money, which he offered him, [the head] of


[p. 83] {#po.11.p83}
الكتاب بينه وبين الملك والشروط لوقت معلوم وزمان محدود وبعث فى ذلك4 الى جميع عملهة فى جميع مدن الملك يامرهم ان يقتل من هناك من7 بنى اسراييل عند بلوغ الوقت والزمان اذا وصل كتابه اليهم واعتد8 خشبة طولبا ماية وعشرين ذراعا ليصلب عليها مرتحى وصيرها" فى منزله وكان فيما بين ذلك10 انه !1 استعد خادمين من خدم الملك كانا يقومان على راس الملك من خاصته وكانا من جنس الاتراك وكان يمال لاحدهما بان ويمال لاخر اتناح وانهما عزما على قتل الملك فى تلك الليلة فعلم بذلك مرتحى عم استير الملكة وتقرّر عنده بالدلايل الواضحة لمثابرته وملازمته دار3! الملك فرفع النصيحة الى الملك واعطلا الدلايل على استعدادهما فلما كان فى الليل وضع الملك عليهما الرصد وهما لا يعلما ان الملك قد عرف قصتهماك! فاخذهما حين عاين الدلايل التى كانت رفت اليه5! فاقرًا له بذلك فامن الملك يقتليما
all the Israelites who were in the cities of his empire. The contract was made between him and the king and the execution of the clauses was fixed for a date determined with precision. Then the governors * of all the cities of the empire received, by Haman's messengers, the order to kill the Israelites who were there * at the expiration of the fixed term, when they would have received the letter. He had a gallows prepared, * 120 cubits high, to hang Mordecai; and he hid this gallows in his house. During that time, two eunuchs of Turkish origin, specially assigned to the bodyguard, one named Bigthan (Ba'thän) and the other Teresh (Itnâh), plotted a conspiracy and resolved to kill the king, the same night. Mordecai, uncle of Queen Esther, learned of this project and discovered clear evidence of it, because he was assiduous and attached to the palace. || He therefore presented a sincere report to Artaxerxes and gave him the evidence of the conspiracy of the two eunuchs. At nightfall, the king had them watched. * They did not know that the king was aware of their deeds and actions. Thus they were caught at the moment when Artaxerxes saw with his own eyes the evidence that had been presented to him. They confessed; then * they were put to death by order of the sovereign.


[p. 84] {#po.11.p84}
وكتب ما كان من نصيحة مرتحى فى كتاب ! اخار الملوك من كتب السير ومكتوب ان الملك ارق فى بعض اليالى فلم يجيه نوم فامر ان يقرا عليه اخبار الملوك من السير فلم يزل الكاتب يقرا عليه الى ان بلغ الى قصة مرتحى ونصيحته3 فقال الملك للكاتب بما ذا كافنا هذا الرجل فقيل للملك انه لم يكافيه الملك بشى4 فامر ان يذكر أمرلا بالغداة ليامس بمكافاته فعند ذلك غليته عينه قنام فلما اصبح دخل عليه هامان وزير وكان اول من يدخل عليه فقال الملك لبامان وزيرلا ما ذا ترى ان يكون مكافاة رجل احب الملك تكرمته وتشريفه7” فقال هامان فى نفسه ليس احد8 اخضش9 به منى فقال مكافاته0! ايها الملك ان يحمل على مركب الملك!! ووضع تاج الملك على راسه وتكون من ياخذ! بلجام دابته مشعرا_بطوف به المدينة وقول هذا"! مكافاة رحجل احب الملك تكرمته وتشريفه فقال له الملك قم4!
What concerns Mordecai's report was recorded in the Book of the Acts of the Kings. It is written that the king, during a night of insomnia when he could not find sleep, had the history of the kings read to him. The secretary continued his reading until he came to the story of Mordecai and his report. Then the king said to the secretary: "What reward have we given to this man?" - "The king has not rewarded him at all," he was answered. He therefore ordered him to be reminded of this matter when morning came, in order to reward Mordecai; and at that moment sleep weighed down his eyelids and he fell asleep. When day came, Haman his minister, who was the first to enter to the king, presented himself: * "What would be, in your opinion, the worthy reward of a man whom the king desires to distinguish and honor?" asked the sovereign. - "No one, more than I, has the king's favor," thought Haman; and he said: "Here, O king, is his reward: one must have this man mount the royal horse, place your own crown on his head; then the one who will hold the bridle of his horse, * having tucked up his clothes, will lead him around the city, * shouting:"


[p. 85] {#po.11.p85}
انت1 من ساعتك واحمل مرتحى على مركب الملك وضع تاج الملك على راسه والزير فى عنقه4 وخذ بلجام دابته وافعل به كالنى قلت فخرج من عند الملك وفعل بمرتحى كالذى امر به الملك فلما انصرف الى منزله حزينا كيبا قالت له امراته ما وزاك وما بالى اراك حزناء كييياة فاخبرها بالقصّة فقالت من اى شعب وامة هذا الرجل فقال لها من بنى اسراييل فذكرت الامراة قديم7 نصرة الله لبنى اسراييل على العمالقة فقالت لبامان أما اذا كان هذا الرجل من شعب بنى اسراييل وبدات ان تقع ونصرع بن يديه فاقبل من الصرعات ما بدا لك8 ثم ان مرتحى تذكر قرب الاجل الذى كان" بين هامان والملك فيما كان ابتاع منه شعب بنى اسراييل ليقتليم9! فبعث الى استير الملكة يسلها ان تكلم الملك!! وردد اليها الرسالة فى ذلك مرارا! ولان سنة الملوك الذى للفرس كانت ان كل من ترايا4! قدام الملك من غير ان
“This is the reward for the man whom the king wishes to distinguish and honor!” - “Rise up immediately,” the king said to him, “have Mordecai mount the royal horse, place my royal crown on his head with a placard at his neck, take the bridle of his mount, and do for him what you have said!” Haman went out from the king’s presence and did for Mordecai what the king had commanded him. When he returned home, sad and desolate, his wife said to him: “What is new? * Why do I see you sad and desolate?” And he told her the story. “Of what people and what nation is this man?” the woman asked. “He is an Israelite,” he replied. The woman remembered that long ago God had granted His help to the Israelites against the Amalekites; and she said to Haman: “Since this man is of the people of the Israelites and you have already begun to fall and humble yourself before him, then accept the humiliations that have befallen you.” Then Mordecai remembered that the term fixed between the king and Haman was approaching, * the term when the Israelites were to be massacred according to the clauses of the agreement. He then had Queen Esther asked to speak to the king, and he repeated his request on this subject several times. The law of the Persian kings required that whoever appeared before the king


[p. 86] {#po.11.p86}
يدعوا به احترًا السيافين راسه الا ان3 يرمى الملك4 بالجرزة الذى بين يديه فيكون ذلك علامة وامان من القتل فكانت استير اذا تذكرت هذا من سنة الملوك تحذر على نفيها وكان مرتحى عتها يلج عليها فى ذلك فلما راها قد تباطأت ببث الها ان كنتى9 لا تفدين نفك بخلاص شعب الرتٍ وامته بنى اسواييل فان الربٍ الاههم يلطف بخلاصهم7 ومع هذا فانك انتى فيمن قد ابتيع9 من الملك19 ودغل فيمن يقتل !1 هامان فلا تثلنّى انك تفلتين13 فبعث14 استير الملكةة! الى مرتحى عمها "تسله16 ان يصوم ويصلّى وباس جميع ابكارة! بنى اسراييل ان يصمن8! مما ثلشة ايام وثلثة ليالى فصامت استير الملكة ثلثة ايام وئاثة لالى فى الصلاة! ولست المسوح وسجدت على الرماد وصلت ودعت الى الله وتضرعت اليه" فى خلاصها من الملك4 وخلاص شعباة من شر هامان فلما كان فى اليوم الرابع بعد ان قضت صومبا لبست استير الملكةة
without being called should have his head cut off by the executioners, unless the king touched with his scepter the one who appeared before him. That was * the sign of [royal] protection. Esther, who remembered this law of the (Persian) kings, feared for her life; but her uncle Mordecai insisted with her. Seeing her hesitation, he had her told: “If you do not redeem your life by saving the people of the Lord, that is to say the Israelites, the Lord who is their God will graciously come to their aid. Moreover, you are among those who are sold by the king, among those whom Haman is to put to death. Do not imagine, therefore, that you will be able to escape.” Then Queen Esther had her uncle Mordecai asked * to fast, to pray, * and to order all the virgins of Israel to fast at the same time as she, for three days and three nights. Queen Esther fasted and prayed for three days and three nights; then she put on sackcloth, * prostrated herself on the ashes, and prayed, begging God with loud cries to preserve her [from the king's anger] and to save her people from the malice of Haman. On the fourth day, when her fast was finished, she put on her royal robes


[p. 87] {#po.11.p87}
لياس الملك وترآت قدام! الملك وقت جلوسه فكانت من احسن النسآ واجملين وبادر السيافون لقتلها فرمى الملك بالجرز؟ نحوها الذنى هو كان علامة الملوك الفرس امان من القتل4 وبادر الملك الها وقدة وقعت على الارض مغشى عليها فقام الملك من مجلسه فاخذها ووضع راسها فى حجر وجعل يسبح وحها واحتملبا حتى احلسها معه فى مجلسه فقال سلى الملك حاجتك فقالت له حاجتى ان يتغدى الملك غدا7 عندى هو وهامان وزير فاحاها الى ذلك وتغدى عندها هو وهامان وزير»" فلما قضا غداة قال لها الملك سلى حاجتك فقالت حاحتى ان يتغدى الملك عندى غدا ايضاة! هو وهامان وزيرل فاحابها الى ذلك وتغدى عندها من غد هو وهامان وزبر فلما قضا غداة قال لها الملك سلى حاحتك فقالت حاجتى ان يتغدى الملك عندى غدا ايضا هو وهامان وزيا فاجابها الى ذلك وتغدى عندها هو وهامان وزير»!! فوقع فى قلب الملك وحشة
and appeared before the king, while he was sitting on the throne. She was one of the prettiest and most graceful women. The officers rushed to kill her. But the king extended his scepter to her, which was among the kings of the Persians the sign of safety. He ran toward her, because she fell to the ground unconscious. Thus the king rose from his throne, took her, placed her head on his chest, and began to caress her face; and, serving as her support, he placed her beside him on his throne. “Ask the king what you desire,” he said to her. “I ask the king to come tomorrow to dine at my house, with his minister Haman,” she replied. * He consented, and the next day he dined at her house with his minister Haman. The meal finished, the king said to Esther: “Ask what you desire.” - “I ask the king and his minister Haman to return tomorrow to dine at my house.” The king accepted; and the next day he and his minister Haman dined at her house. After dinner the king said to her: “Ask what you desire.” She replied: “I ask that the king and his minister Haman dine again at my house tomorrow.” The king accepted, and the next day he and his minister Haman dined at her house once more. But a cooling and a feeling of jealousy toward his minister Haman crept into the


[p. 88] {#po.11.p88}
وغيرة من هامان وزيرلا فلما قضا غداة قال لبا سلى! الملك حاجتك قالت استير الملكة واى حاحة تكون لمن قد بلغ .اجل قتلبا وفنا حياتها واستئصال شعها وامتها فقال ليا الملك وقد بلغ منه الحزن والغعّ كل3 غاية ومن الذى فعل هذا قالت له4 من حسدك علتى ولم يحب ان يكون لك مثلى هامان وزيرك هذا فاستطلق بطن الملك من الع والحزن وقام الى المخرج9 مادرً ثم خرج وراى7 هامان يتضرع الى استير الملكة ويقمل اسفل8 رحليها فراه؟ الملك حيث خرج فقال له وقد اخذته الغيرة وايضا هاة! انت تطلب عيبنا وسمع الله صلوة مرتحى وقبل صيامه وصلوة!! استير الملكة وصلوة جميع ناكا بنى اسرابيل فامر الملك بصلب هامان الوّيرة! ورفع الى الملك ان هامان قد اا لمرتحى خشبة طولبا ماية وعشزين ذراع ليصلبه عليبها فامر الملك ان يصلب
heart of the king. After dinner he said to Esther: “Ask what you desire.” Queen Esther replied: “What request could she make who is on the point of being put to death and losing her life, * she whose people are about to be exterminated?” The king, at the height of sadness and affliction, said to her: * “But who then has done this?” She replied to him: “It is he who is jealous of you because of me and who does not want you to have a wife like me—it is your minister Haman, here present.” At that moment, the sadness and affliction so loosened the sovereign's bowels that he had to hasten to go out. Then he came out (of the latrines) and saw Haman * prostrating himself before Queen Esther and kissing the tips of her feet. Seized with jealousy, the king cried out: “What! You want to dishonor us even now?” Thus God had heard the prayer of Mordecai, accepted his fast as well as the prayer of Queen Esther and all the Israelite women. The king gave orders for Haman to be hanged; and someone having reported to him that Haman had had a gallows erected, 120 cubits high, to have Mordecai hanged there, * he


[p. 89] {#po.11.p89}
هامان على تلك الخشبة! وخظص الله بنى اسراييل وسلمهم من مكيدة هامان العمليقى ومن بعد ارطحششت الطويل اليدين ملك3 ارطحششت الثانى بعده سنتين ثم ملك من بعد سغدينوس سنة ومن بعد ذلك داربوش نونس تمع عشر اسنة
ordered that his minister would be hanged on that gallows. It is thus that God saved the Israelites and snatched them from the treachery of Haman the Amalekite. After Artaxerxes Longimanus, Artaxerxes II reigned * for two years; after him, Sogdianus reigned for one year, and after him, Darius Nothus for 19 years.
وفى ذلك الزمان صار اقليدس9 صاحب البندسة7 وفى سنة خسة من ملك داربوش خالف اهل مصر على الفرس فقاموا لهم ملكا من بعد ماية وارعة وعشرين سنة استعدوهم؟
At that time, lived Euclid, the geometer. In the 5th year of the reign of Darius, the inhabitants of Egypt revolted against the Persians and, after having been under their yoke for 124 years, gave themselves a king.

At the same time, flames coming out of Mount Etna consumed a large number of cities. In that time, the Greeks learned * twenty-four letters; they had only sixteen before. * Cadmus and Agenor, who were from Egypt, had come to the city of Athens and had brought there the sixteen characters, with which the Greeks wrote at first. Here are these characters:

These characters are called Phoenician characters. Then Palamedes, from the city of Argos, invented four other characters, which are these: ΖΘΗΧ. Finally, Simonides invented four other characters, namely: ΜΞΟΨ. It is said that the Egyptians invented the alphabet first and that the Phoenicians (learned it) after them. These were the letters that Cadmus had long ago brought * into that country. Then the Greeks (learned the alphabet) after the Phoenicians.

At that time, [the Greeks] put Socrates to death: they made him drink poison and he died. After him, his disciples Plato and Epicurus distinguished themselves, and they taught diverse doctrines.

In the 15th year of King Artaxerxes, Africanus (Afriquiyous) invaded the country of Carthage (Carchedona) and seized it; after which this country was called * Africa, from the name of Africanus. Then Artaxerxes II reigned for 27 years.

At that time, the numbering and census of the inhabitants of Rome took place; this census lasted for three years; and it was interrupted due to the fatigue of those who were doing it. In that time, Nectanebo (Fastou), king of Egypt, fled to Ethiopia, because, according to the augurs, * numerous armies were to descend upon him. Some people say that he was the father of Alexander. The empire of the Egyptians was annihilated for 42 years, until the time of Ptolemy, one of the servants of Alexander, son of Philip.

In the 4th year of the reign of King Artaxerxes, Aristotle, aged 17, was at the school of Plato.

At that time, Philip, father of Alexander, began to reign in Macedonia; the name of his mother was Olympias. Then Darius, son of Arsaces, reigned * for 6 years. In the first year of his reign, Alexander, son of Philip, the Macedonian, ascended the throne. He was 20 years old. He reigned first over Hellas for 12 years * and he lived 32 years. He subdued many regions and, after having put thirty-five kings to death, he seized their states; his army was composed of one hundred and twenty thousand men; he was three cubits tall.

he invaded the [various] countries, making war on the kings of the entire world and seizing their states. He arrived * thus in the region of Sind * and seized it; from there he prepared to invade India. He therefore sent a part of his troops, and at their head one of his lieutenants named Seleucus, to call the Indians to obedience. Seleucus had orders to obtain their spontaneous submission and, if they resisted, to fight them. Arrived at the border of India—it was at the beginning of winter—Seleucus wrote to the Indians what Alexander had ordered him regarding them, and had his letter carried by three notables of his army. The reading of this letter caused the Indians a fear mixed with stupor; but they answered negatively to the proposals of Seleucus, * and let him know that he was not in a state to fight their troops. They detained his envoys until they had arranged their troops; after which they set out at the head of a multitude so numerous that one has never seen the like. They brought a crowd

of elephants, from the top of which they fought. They met Seleucus, who had not moved from his camp and who did not have time to arrange [his troops] in battle order. He nevertheless went out with his army, and the two parties came to blows. But the cavalry of Seleucus, seized with fear at the sight of the elephants, * dispersed and returned to the camp. Seleucus then wrote to Alexander to tell him about his situation and that of the Indians, * about their elephants which had put his cavalry to rout, and to ask him if he

should restart the battle. * Alexander, having read the letter of Seleucus, summoned his philosophers, communicated this letter to them, and asked their advice in this regard. They said by common agreement that he should abstain from war during the rest of the winter, to undertake it himself afterward. Then he wrote to Seleucus, commander of his army, and ordered him to stay where he was, in the country of Sind, during the rest of the winter. At this news, the Indians, who only asked to leave the Greeks alone, moved away. Alexander took advantage of this delay to immediately make preparations: he gathered the workers who knew how to work bronze and ordered them to prepare forty hollow elephants of bronze, * similar to the elephants of the

Indians, and to fill them with coal. The feet of these elephants were removable * and could be assembled [at will]; underneath were adapted wheels on which they could advance. Alexander appointed guards to prevent anyone from approaching them and seeing them, because he had his reasons for hiding them from people's eyes. They began to write to him without letting anything of their fears appear and started discussions, seeing that he had philosophers with him. The king of India gave him a * wonderful gift and sent him a letter in which he heaped praise on the Indian philosophers who were with him. Then Alexander ordered his philosophers to reply. A correspondence began between them and differences of opinion did not take long to appear in their ranks. The two kings entered into correspondence to organize a meeting of these philosophers. * They recalled in their letters in what consisted the dissent between Socrates,

as we have explained, Hippocrates, Democritus, Aristotle, and their followers. The meeting of the philosophers was fixed for a day and an hour, * to explain what they had disputed about. And they gathered for this purpose.
فكانت رسالة ملك البندة الى الاسكندر فى ذلك هكذا لعظيم الملوك الاسكندر من ملك البند وساي الرعيه اما بعد التحية فشملتك ليها الملك النعمة فكملت لك الحكمة ودامت لك السلامة واجتمعت لك9 السبطة وارنفعت لك الدرجة واستغزرت لك الفاسفة وانخفضت7 لك العامة وفتح لك ما اغلق البلا واغلق عنك ما فتح البلا واجرى يفك رغايب الدنيا وتحطمت عنك9 نوايب المكرود وب امينًا!! لطوارق! الشر وتناظمت لك حرن المملكة وارتفعت بك امال الناس3! واحاطت بحافتك غرر! الاولاد وقتريت15 لك الحياة براحة النفس ما استحيت الحبوة؟! وبتمام7!
Here is the letter from the king of India to Alexander on this subject: “To Alexander, the greatest of kings, from the king of India and all his subjects, greetings! May happiness, O king, envelop you; may your wisdom be perfect; * may your health last a long time; may the earth belong to you entirely; may your rank be high; may philosophy flourish with you; may your people be submissive; may that which misfortune closes be opened for you, and may that which misfortune opens be closed for you; may all the desirable things of the world come into your hand; * may the blows of fortune be blunted far from you; may you be sheltered from the surprises of evil; may the defense of your kingdom be well organized; may the hopes of your subjects increase through you; * may elite children come to surround you; may your life be strengthened by the tranquility of the soul as long as you love life and by the fullness of intelligence until the end of the world; may your life be prolonged in health and may your troops be victorious until the borders of your empire; may you


[p. 97] {#po.11.p97}
العقل امدا البقا وتطول العمر فى سلامة المحيا" وبع الجنود الى اخر المملكة مع تظاهر الكلام ببهاء الحكمة وانبساط الدنيا بجميع8 الرغايب واستحكام القدرة بتمام الغز وبركة التوفيق فى رجال العمل وتعرف النجاح باصابة الرآى وهر البموم بمعرفة التدير واخذ الامان من نوايب الغيرة دعونا لك بهذا مع غلماننا وتقتمناة اليك بالنيئة لما سموت به عندنا7 زعموا ان الارض تمكنك من نواحببا وان العامة تنقاد لك8 باجمعها وان سلطانك يفوق اهل زمانك من ملوك الارض وان العاقة تسكن عنك حد الاعدا ولا ينالك9 خضطة0! السلطان وانك تملك شعب الملوك الاربعة ويكون ذلك فى عقب القرون كبيئة ملك قد نشا فى ابنا!! الاعاجم وكعزة نمروذ “ ابن كنعان فى ساي الملوك حتى يضى لك بوقيد الملوك! كالبرق وشت لك دعايمه كالجال "!ا ويحما وقوده كالنار وبصلب قوته كالحديد ويكون فى ملك الملوك وعابر الدهر4! كبيات المناقع الاربعة الما والنار والارض والبوا فهذه16 كانت رسالة
speak with the brilliance of wisdom; may you enjoy this lower world with all its goods; may your power be strengthened by the height of your might; may God help you to find experienced aides; may you succeed * in the accuracy of your plans; may your worries be overcome by the experience of government; may you be sheltered from the vicissitudes of fate! We make these wishes for you with our subjects and we warn you with our greetings because of our high opinion of you. It is said that the earth gives you power over its regions; that all peoples obey you; that your power is superior to that of the kings of the earth, your contemporaries; that the result [of your enterprises] soothes the envy of your enemies; that your power cannot be broken; that you reign over the peoples of the four kings; * and that you will reign thus until the end of the centuries, [great] like the king who has already risen among the Persians, and powerful like Nimrod, * son of Canaan, among all the other kings; so that your empire will shine with the splendor of kings like lightning; that its foundations will be solid like the mountains; that its burnt offerings will burn like fire, and that its strength will be firm like iron; that it will live among the other kings, until the end of the centuries, like the four elements: water, fire, earth, and air.” Such was the tenor of this letter.


[p. 98] {#po.11.p98}
فامى الاسكندرا فلاسفته فردوا جوابً ونقصوا بعض ما ذكروا من خطة السلطان فجرت بينهم فى ذلك من صفة الاخلاط وكان جواب الاسكندر هذالا
By order of Alexander, his philosophers replied, but they abbreviated a part of the title of the king (of India), for * their opinion on this subject was different. Here is the reply of Alexander:
من والى ملك الزيان وعظيم الارض ودعمتها4 الاسكندر الى ملك البند أما بعد التسمية فاعطيت نضار الها وزينت بحظوة الملك وبلغت غاية الصلاح واشتهرت بسطة الع وكانت عليك يد تكلاوك9 ومدّ لك فى دوام المملكة وذلل لك ناحية الارض وتفرغت سالفة الملوك واعطيت بقية الملك والدهر7 وكان الملكانة مع تشاغليهما بمكاتبتهما وامساكبهما عن الحرب لم يدعا المكايدة نظراً؟ لانفسهما فى التحّز0! والحذر والاحتياط !! فى اقامة شانهما وان ملك البند كاتب ملك الصين فيما حدث عليه من غزو! الاسكندر ايلا ووصف له النى كان من امرهما وانذره قدومه عليه وحذّر غزود"! ايلا من بعد ان هو ظفر به14
“From Alexander, the greatest king of the era * and of the earth, the support of the world, to the king of India. You have received an unmixed splendor; you are adorned with royal dignity and you have arrived at the highest degree of virtue; you are famous for the greatness of your power. May there be a hand upon you that guards you; may your empire be lasting; may the earth be submissive to you; may you rid yourself of the previous kings, and may all power be given to you until the end of the centuries!” * The two kings, while they were occupied with their correspondence and * abstained from war, did not disdain to use ruses, one toward the other, keeping on their guard, and watching over the state of their affairs. The king of India entered into correspondence with the king of China; he let him know that Alexander had attacked him, and told him what had happened between them; he warned him to take care that Alexander did not attack him, when he had won the victory over (himself); he asked him for help, assistance, and


[p. 99] {#po.11.p99}
وعرض ! عليه الموازرة والمعاونة والمكانفة لما رحا ان يصير اليه من ذلك من الع والقتوة لما فيه فبخ لقوة عزة الاسكندر وكسن الانابهة وردع لاقحامة وصرفه عنهما7 ان اتفق8 رابهما واجتنت كلمتهما
support, hoping thus to have strength and power to make up for his weakness before the great force of Alexander, to overcome his resistance, * to push him to leave his country and to move away if they agreed and came to an understanding.
فلما قرا ملك الصين كتابه9 وتدبيرة0! وعرف!! صواب ما كتب به من الراى! وما دعلا اليه فرد عليه جوابه بالانعام ورحاة! فى ذلك الخير4! وعرف! فيه صواب الراى واعلمه انه بادى6!. يعمل فى تهبية”! ذلك وموث على كل عمل ونتبى فيه الى اقصا الغاية من مقدرته ويسله ان يوذنه8! عند وقت الزمان الذى يحتاج فيه الى9! نجدته
When the king of China had read his letter, and knew his way of thinking, as well as the accuracy of his designs of which he wrote, and of his request, he sent him a favorable reply; for he had the hope of drawing his profit from all that; and he recognized, in his letter, the advantages of this enterprise. * He let him know that he was beginning his preparations, that he was ready to set to work actively and to employ all his power; [at the same time] he asked him to inform him of the moment when he would need his help.
فلغ الاسكندر قستهما وقد استعدٌ وفرغ من صناعاته اجمع فاذن بالرحيل وبعد ايام قلايل شخص بساكره حتى انتبى الى بلاد البند! ونزل مسكر سليقون خليفته
Alexander learned * these news, while he had already made his preparations and finished all his work. A few days after having announced the departure, he left with his troops, arrived at the country of India, and halted at the camp of Seleucus his lieutenant. He wrote to the king of India, to warn him and invite him


[p. 100] {#po.11.p100}
وكتب الى صاحب البند يعلمه ويدعوه الى طاعته ويوذنه! بالحرب ان هو كرا ذلك وهيّا لكتابه رسلا صير نفسه احدهم سر إرادة3 للمعاينة لموضع4 برتادهة لقتليم؟ وللقياهم فيه فى ممرا فى. البلاد وبمعرفته بامرهم7 وما .ير عليه من8 حاليم وان صاحب" البند احسن انزاليم واسنى الطافهم"! وعجل اصرافهم بخلاف ما دعلا اليه!! الاسكندر واعلمه فى احابته انه لا ينتظر قدومه عليه حتى يثيّن بساكرن اليه12 وان صاحب البند عند ذلك كتب الى ملك الصين يعلمه ما اتلا: من راى الاسكندر ويسله التعجيل بنجدته كما كان كتب اليه وان الاسكندر اراد لمعسكر موضا على موافقته! بينه وبين مستقر4! ملك البند ثالثون فرسخا فلم يابث حين وصل الى مسكر ان5! اذن بالرحيل؟! مبادراً فى عدة كثيرة من اتراك واسواق”! حتى نزل ذلك الموضع واعجل صاحب18 البند لدنون منه عند انتظار نجدة صاحب الصين فلغ ذلك
to make his submission; * otherwise, he would declare war on him. He had the letter carriers prepared; and Alexander himself joined them secretly, because he wanted to see with his own eyes the place, to study it, to fight the Indians when he would pass through their country, and to learn their position and realize their situation. The king of India received them with kindness and heaped favors upon them; but he dismissed them quickly, * contrary to what Alexander would have desired, and let him know in his reply that he would not wait for the arrival of Alexander, but that he would appear * with his troops before him. In the meantime, the king of India wrote and let the king of China know the intention that Alexander had conceived, and asked him to come faster with the help about which he had written to him. Alexander chose for his army an advantageous place, at a distance of 30 parasangs from the residence of the king of India. As soon as he arrived in the camp of his army, he pressed the departure and set out on the march with a great quantity of provisions and food, camped in * that place, and pushed the king of India to approach him, while he was still waiting for the help of the king of China. Learning this, the king of India


[p. 101] {#po.11.p101}
صاحب البند فكتب اليه بوعده وبهدده فشخص بعسكرةا حتى نزل قربا على ميل! من مسكر الامكندر وكان موضعه مطميئا عن مسكر الامكندرة وكان موضع الامكندر مشرفا عليه
wrote a threatening letter to Alexander to intimidate him. Then he set out at the head of his troops and camped at a distance of one mile from Alexander's army. The location of his camp was lower than that of Alexander's camp, which was on a height.
وان الاسكندر “ اذنه باللقا من غد وعبّا له فيلة5 النحاس وحشاها الفحم وعند الليل5 اشعل النار فيها فلم يصبح7 حتى حميت وتاججت نارها لا يستطيع احد الدنوة منها لشدة دهج حرارتها9 فلما اصبح وارتفع النهار بكر البندى !! بساكربهة! فبدره الحيلة؟! واقهل الى معسكر الاسكندر على بختة14 وكانت معه فيلة كثيرة عليها مقاتلةة! امام عسكرا قلما دنت فبلته8! وعاينت فيلة الاسكندرة! فلتت البها! وظن فرسانها"! انها فيلة المند الثى لم تتادب للقتال فحملوا عليها على غرَةٍ! وان الاسكندر أمر بدفع فيلته من خلف
Alexander * let him know that he wanted to go to meet him in the morning. He arranged his bronze elephants in order, filled them with coal, and, during the night, lit a fire in them. It was not yet day when the lit fire produced such intense heat that no one could approach the elephants. When day came, * the king of India, who had risen early in the morning with his troops, was surprised by the stratagem: he approached Alexander's camp unexpectedly, having at the front of his army several elephants, mounted by warriors. When his elephants had approached * and had seen Alexander's elephants, they engaged in combat with them, because the riders who were on the elephants believed that they were elephants from Sind, which were not accustomed to combat; and the Indians attacked them imprudently. Then Alexander had his elephants pushed from behind straight against them; they began to move and ran on their


[p. 102] {#po.11.p102}
فى وجوههم فخرجت! با تجرى تلك البكرات مسرعة لاشراف موضعا واتحدارها فمالا لثت افيلة البند ان شدت عليبا كالعادة فى قتلباك فسطعتها بمشافرها فاشتعات احتراقاة فوت عند ذلك منهزمة لا تنثنى ولا يملك سواقها من امرها شيا شاردة لا تلقا فارسً ولا راجلا من البنديين الا طحطحته من شدة الم الاحتراق فانتفضت لذلك7 نتة البنديين وعند ذلك انبزم البندى وصاك:؟ وان اصحاب الاسكندر طلبوهم فى كل وادى وتلعة9 فقتلوهم واكثروا صرعاهم "! وعند ذلك جات للبندى تجدة الصينتق!! مرحفين كينا قد حفيت3ا دوابهم فووا فى عسكرهم ليس هم حراك4! ولا عندهم غناةً! وان الاسكندر غبى عنه قصتهم وظن حيث راى17 ممسكرهم انها حيلة منهم18 فجمع فلاسفته فال لهم قد ترون سرعة
wheels with great speed, because of the height and the slope of the place. The elephants of the king of India did not delay in throwing themselves upon them, as they were accustomed to doing during combat, and struck Alexander's elephants with their trunks, * but they burned themselves and took flight without looking back * and without it being possible for their drivers to stop them; suffering horribly from their burns, they crushed all the Indian cavalrymen and infantrymen they encountered; this threw the Indian cavalry into disorder, so that the king of India and his troops took flight. Alexander's troops pursued them in all directions and killed a very large number of them. In the meantime, the auxiliary troops of the king of China, agitated and exhausted, arrived at the king of India's camp, with their tired pack animals. They stopped in the Indian camp without movement * and without resources. Alexander, who was unaware of their situation, imagined, after having seen their camp, that it was a ruse on their part. || He therefore gathered his philosophers


[p. 103] {#po.11.p103}
مادتهم لقربها منهم وما نحن فيه من الذوب وانه لا مادة لنا كمادتهم قد امسينا امن عشا » نرى! قتليم وانّا قد ابدناهم3 فلم يصبح حتى عاد عسكرهم احفل4 مما كان اولاة مما ترون فى ذلك وفى امرنا وحالنا فيينما هم كذلك فى التفكر اذ قال كبير فلاسفتهم لنت ارى لنا لقاء او يوم نخرج لقتالهم دون .يوم الثلثا وكان ذلك من قوله يوم الارما قبله بسبعة ايام
and said to them: "You already see with what speed their reinforcements have arrived and in what state of exhaustion we are; [you see] that we have fewer resources than they have. Yesterday, at nightfall, we witnessed their massacre * and we made them perish. But barely had the day risen when their army returned more numerous than before. What is your opinion on this, on our situation, and on our position?" While they were reflecting, the oldest of their philosophers said: "I believe * that we must attack them and fight them next Tuesday." * Now this word was spoken on Wednesday, seven days before the
كان ملك البند فى شد من اغتمامه بمناهضتهم من شت حال الصينيين8 ووجا9 دوااهم فدعان ذلك الى جمع حتابه0! فنظروا فى مثل الذى نظرل! فيه الاسكندر فى الامساك عن القتال ومسلته المناظرة فنما هو افضل وارفق من القتال فوافق كتاهم وات هوا من! الاسكندر فى الامساك عن القتال ولما راى وعاين من امرهم وان الاسكندر دعا فلاسفته فقال ليم اتوا القوم! واسمعوا منهم وناظروهم واعرقوا ازاوهم! " ثم تمل
Tuesday. The king of India was extremely distressed by the arrival of the Chinese because of their misery and the fatigue of their mounts. For this reason, he gathered his astrologers who examined the same question as Alexander, concerning the suspension of the war and the request he had made to examine what was better and easier than war. Their letter and their request agreed with the desire Alexander had for a suspension of arms, because he had seen their situation with his own eyes. Then he called * his philosophers and said to them: "Go visit the enemies, listen to them, engage in a dispute with them, and try to know their intentions; * then we will act


[p. 104] {#po.11.p104}
يقدر ما نرى ففعلوا ما امرهم به واتصرقوا يما سمعوا منهم ودعوهم اليه واتصرفوا معهم! فلاسفة البنديين ليسمعوا جواب الاسكندر فى ذلك وقد كان اع لهم الامكندر فى ذلك3 رجالاً من وجو اصحابه وقرسانه وامرجم بلقلبهم براى كلاما القاد الهم وانهم لقوهم به واعلموهم انهم فى شد من صنع الاسكندر بموافاته بهم وتعرضه لمملكتمة فان الفيقين جميعا لفى خطا مبين وظللة” ظاهرة فى قتليم بعضيم بعضاة فى هذين الملكين ففعلوا كما أمرهم به الاسكتدر واعلموهم9 ردلا عليهم
accordingly." They executed the order received; and after having listened to the Indians, they left, inviting them to come and find Alexander. Then the Indian philosophers accompanied them to hear Alexander's response on this subject. Now, the latter had prepared for them some of his notables and cavalrymen to whom he had suggested his design and his words. The meeting took place under these conditions. [The Greeks] told the Indians that they were very distressed * by Alexander's approach, by his arrival among them, and by the fact that he had invaded their kingdom, for both parties were in an obvious error and in a manifest delusion, by massacring one another because of these two kings. They acted as Alexander had ordered them, and gave them his response.
فلما سمع البنديون منطتهم وافقهم0! فقالوا ان!! الراى فى ذلك فقال الاخرون! ملككم حبار من الجابرة فى جسمه وفروسيته وصاحبنا قصير حقير ليس له فروسية3ا فلو اجتمعتم على اتفاق من الراى والبواك! ودعوتم صاحبكم الى مبادرةة! صاحنا ونحن تفعل كمثل ذلك بصاحنا فلا بد ان6! تكون الغلبة لاحدهما قنستريح نحن7! وائتم من هذا القتال
Upon hearing these words, the Indians agreed with them and said that it was also their opinion. Others (companions of Alexander) said: "Your king is very powerful by his physical strength and by his art of training horses; and our king is small in stature and puny, and he does not know the art of training horses. If you have the same designs * and the same desires, urge your king to fight in single combat * with our king, we will do the same * with regard to the latter, and one of them will inevitably


[p. 105] {#po.11.p105}
والبلا! وان البنديين فارقوهم على هذا وام يشكواة عن ضميرهم فيه فدرهم ووثقودة وقالوا لهم اعملوا انتم ونحن فى ذلك9 فاخذوا الجواب كتابهم من7 الاسكندر وانصرفوا وام يون ليم عليه"
be the victor. Then we will rest, we and you, from this war and this calamity." The Indians left after having accepted these conditions and not doubting at all the sincerity of the Greeks; this confidence filled them with joy, and they said to the Greeks: "Let us set to work, both of us!" Having taken the response that Alexander had made to their letter, they left, without having obtained an audience with the latter.
فلما دنوا معسكرهم دنوا من اصحايم9؟ بهذا الراى فوافئق الهوا فيه العامة0! ولم يشكوا!! ان الغلية3! لصاحبهم ملك البند؟! ان اجتمعاً! والذى دعا الاسكندر الى هذا الراى والجراة عليه علمه! بان احله ليس ياتيه دون رجوعه ونزوله ريمية لا بشك7! فيه ولذلك كان حب لنفسه فلم تزل البنديون بملكهم! حتى احاب الى مبادرة"! الاسكندر وعلى ان الملك لمن غلب فتكانالة فى ذلك واستوئق بعضهما بعض وابعدوا اللقا!؟ جميعاً الى يوم السابع الذى كان فى حسام وهو يوم الثلثا
When they had arrived at their camp, they shared this project with their companions, which had the general assent of the multitude. They did not doubt that the victory would be for the king of India, their master, if the meeting took place. What pushed Alexander to this audacious enterprise was his conviction that he would only die after his return and his arrival in Rome; he had no doubt in this regard: he therefore trusted in himself. The Indians did not cease to insist with their king until he had agreed to fight in single combat with Alexander, and this, on the condition that the empire would belong to the victor. * They entered into correspondence on this subject, made their agreements, and, by common accord, postponed the meeting to the seventh day, * which, according to their calculation, was a Tuesday.


[p. 106] {#po.11.p106}
وان الاسكندر امر اصحابه بالتبية وهيّا له مزراقا كانت فروسيته فيه لا تخطى شى! ثم خرج ببسكر واقبل الاخر بعسكر حتى اصطقّت الفرقين3 جميعاً وبرذ الملكان من بين الصفين كان الاسكندر يكثر الكلام بالبندية5 فقال الاسكندر للبندى؟ انت حبار من الجابرة وانا قصير حقير وقد خفت منى7 وهالك8 امرى قد استعنت9 على بغيرك معك10 فقال البندى وقد اغضه قوله!! والى من احتاج1 استعين به عليك ققال له الاسكندر فهذا الفارس خلفك13 فلتفت البندى لينظر من خلفه فوشقه! الاسكندر. بمزراقه فى رقبتهة! فلم بخط مذابحهة! حيث لوى عنقه الى خلف"! فوقع ميتا عن فرسه واحتملونهة! اصحابه"! وانصرف الفريقين الى مسكرهم
Alexander ordered his companions to prepare for him a short lance, in the handling of which he was as skillful as he was never lacking. Then he went out with his troops, and the other arrived with his, and the two armies lined up in good order. The two kings left the ranks. Alexander, who spoke the language of the Indians, said to the king of India: "You are a giant, while I am small and puny; yet you fear me and my position terrifies you; you have asked others for help against me." The king of India, irritated by these words, said: "From whom do I need to implore * help against you?" Alexander answered him: * "From the horseman who is behind you!" The king of India having turned around to look at who was behind him, Alexander pierced his neck with a lance blow: thus he dealt him a mortal blow, when the latter turned his head. || He fell dead from his horse * and his companions carried him away. The two armies returned to their camp.
فبعث الاسكندر الى البنديين فاتود ضخطب عليهم وقال ليم ما لى راى فى المقام90 فى بلادكم ولا همتى !3 اخذ الاموال منكم ولا اسلكم غير الطاعة لى وان تحملوا التى
Alexander then ordered the Indians to come and find him and he gave them this speech: "I have no intention of staying in your country, nor of taking your goods; I ask of you only obedience. If you bring me every year, on this same day, a bag of earth * from your country, that will be for me the sign of


[p. 107] {#po.11.p107}
حيث! كنت فى كل عام فى مثل هذا اليوم حرا فيه ترابكم من بلادكم فهو علامة طاعتكم عندى فانصرفوا واختاروا لكم ملكا وملكود عليكم ثم انصرف عنهم فاعجب البنديين قوله وسرهم منطقه وهجوا له3 وانصرفوا الى اصحاهم وسرت4 العامة يما اتوهم به فحملوا الى الاسكندر البديا وحشدوا عليه طرايف بلادهم واكثروا له قرة المواكب واعظموا له العطية واخبروه بموضع الصينيين عندهم وسالوه إن يفعل بهم كما قعل بالبنديين7 وان قم ملك على حاله فاجابهم الى ذلك وملك عليم صاحبيم الذى إختارئ ثم رحل9 عنهم وجال فى بلاد حوليم وخلفهم حتى اتبى ثم انصرف الى بابل فينما هم يسيرون"! على ظهر!! الطريق اذ وجده قترة! وتاذى من حرارة الشمس قزل عن فرسه وظلل4! اصحابه عليه باتراسهم! وهى مملوة17 بالذهب فلم يجد حقّة18 وقرب ذهاب النبار فامرهم ان يعدّلوا به9! الى اقرب القرى منه0 ففعلوا به كما أمرهم !* ونات
your submission. Go, choose yourselves a king and make him reign over you." Then Alexander left them. The Indians, surprised by his language, cheered and rejoiced at his speech, returned to their companions, and everyone rejoiced at the news they brought. Then they brought Alexander gifts, amassed for him the rarest things of their country, sent him many sumptuous processions, and showered him with presents. They indicated to him the place in their country where the Chinese were camped and asked Alexander to treat them in the same way. They asked him furthermore to give them a king of his choosing. || He consented and gave them as king the one among them * that they had chosen. Then Alexander left them, traveled through the country around them * and behind them, up to the limits. After which he left for Babylon. During this march through the desert, Alexander felt weakness and became ill from the heat of the sun: he got down from his horse. His companions placed him in the shade of their shields covered with gold. But he did not experience any improvement. Toward the end of the day, he ordered them to lead him to the nearest village. They did as he had ordered them, and he spent the night there. At the dawn of the day, the illness had worsened; * he asked * for the name of


[p. 108] {#po.11.p108}
بها مقيماً واصبح وقد اشتدت به الشكاية! فسال عن اسم القرية فاخبروا انها تسما رومية المداين فاتقطع عند ذلك رجاو بيحسابه كان بان ميتته تدركه فى ايت من ذهب برومية فلما شمخن3 بدا بالكلام والوصية ولم يكن له وارث3 قصين وصيته الى سليقوس خليفته واستخلفه على بابل فادركه اجله فى ذلك الموضع لتمام اثنين وثلثين سنة.عاشها فى الدنيا ملك فيها اننا عشر سنة وقال ان بعض عبيدة سقاا سما فى موضعه فقتله وان بطلميوس النى ملك بعد حمله4 فدقنه فى الاسكندرة فلما بلغ عبيده وفانه غلب كل واحد منهم على الموضع الى كان استخلقه عليها منهم بطلميوس ابن ارنبة ملك مص ارين سنة وملك فليفوس مقدونية” وانطيغونية وملك ديمطريوس8 الثام واسيا وتقدم سليقوس الى مصر فى سنة الثالث عشر من سنى بطلميوس ابن ارنب وغلب على الثام وطلب9 ديمطريوس الى بلاد اسيا فقتله واحتوى! علها مع الغام وابل وملكبا!! اثنين وثلثين سنة
this village, and when it was reported to him that it was called Roumiya al-Madaïn (Rome), he lost all hope, persuaded that he was predestined that death must strike him in Rome in a house of gold. Feeling defeated by the illness, he spoke to make his testament; and as he had no heirs, he bequeathed in favor of Seleucus, his lieutenant, whom he designated to succeed him in Babylon. Death struck Alexander in this place. He had lived 32 years in this lower world and reigned for twelve years. It is said that one of his servants made him drink poison in this locality and thus caused him to die. * Ptolemy, who reigned after him, carried him * to Alexandria and buried him there. When his officers (lit. his servants) had learned the news of his death, each of them seized the region where he had established them as lieutenants: among them, there was Ptolemy, son of Lagos, who reigned over Egypt for 40 years; Philip who reigned over Macedonia and Antigonia; Demetrius who reigned over Syria and Asia; Seleucus who advanced against Egypt in the 13th year of Ptolemy, son of Lagos, who conquered * Syria and, having pursued Demetrius to Asia, killed him. || He was master of Asia, Syria, and Babylon where he reigned for 32 years.

At this time, Antiochus built Antioch and gave it his name. In that time, Seleucus built Apamea, Aleppo, Kinnesrin, Edessa (ar-Rohä), Seleucia, and Laodicea. The famous Simeon, son of Jechonias, was then high priest of the Jews; he had as successor his brother Eleazar
وفى سنة تسعة من ملك بطلميوس دبّرةٌ البهود من انطيوخس العظيم وانه جا الى بلاد يهوذا التى هم ها واستعدهم ” وق سنة احدي عش فى بطلميوس اففاس9" وضط9! مداين سورية وبلدان بنى اسراييل حيث ببث انقوس !! ريسن حيشه فخرج مقابله انطيوض الكبير فى سنة احد عش من ملك بطلميوس وان انطيوخس حارب الروم فغلبوه واخذوا الروم منه ابنه! انطيوخس اففانس رهينة13 الى مدينة رومية14 فاقام لهم ان يعطيهم فى كل سنة الف ككر مال
In * the 9th year of the reign of Ptolemy, * Antiochus the Great subjugated the Jews: having come to Judea where they lived, he made them slaves. In the 11th year, Ptolemy Epiphanes, having sent Scopas, one of his generals, besieged the cities of Syria and Judea and seized them. * Antiochus the Great went to meet him, in the 11th year of Ptolemy's reign. Antiochus then made war on the Romans who defeated him and took as a hostage, in the city of Rome, his son Antiochus Epiphanes. He agreed to give them every year a thousand talents of silver

Ptolemy, in the 13th year * of his reign, made a peace treaty with Antiochus who gave him his daughter Cleopatra in marriage, and Ptolemy received from him Syria and Phoenicia as a dowry. In the 17th year of Ptolemy, Antiochus the Great was killed in the land of Persia; he was stoned in the temple of a god. After him, his son reigned * for 12 years. At this time, Simon or Simeon addressed a prayer to Apollonius, head of the army in Phoenicia, promising to give him much gold. When Seleucus had learned * by the report of his general Apollonius that there was much gold in the Temple of Jerusalem, he sent an army. But God punished him, and he was put to death. In the 131st year of the Greeks, Ptolemy Philometor ascended the throne and reigned 35 years. At this time, flourished Aristobulus, who translated [into Greek] for King Philometor the Torah

In the 6th year of Ptolemy and 137th of the Greeks, * at the indiction when Antiochus, son of Antiochus, began to reign - that one had been a hostage in the city of Rome - Antiochus advanced to make war on Ptolemy, king of Egypt. Prevented by the Romans, he returned to Judea and gave the sovereign pontificate to Jason (Sim'oun), brother of Onias, son of Jechonias. * Then he took it away from him and gave it to Onias, surnamed Menelaus. This disagreement which broke out between them was a cause of evils and afflictions for the Jews

It was a statue of Zeus * Olympian. He also placed (another) statue of Zeus Xenios, on Mount
